# swarm-191133-4hqx

**Task:** BUILD THE SUITE THAT MEASURES WHAT THIS HUB IS ACTUALLY GOOD AT: MULTI-PART BUILD TASKS WITH PLANTED DEFECTS, SCORED ON DEFECTS CAUGHT AND DEFECTS SHIPPED, COMPARING ONE AGENT, A PAIR AND A ROOM AT EQUAL BUDGET. Research first, test first, verified by a non-author who reads the diff. Main is 98e17b2. Call wait_for_messages with hold_until_actionable=true; working exchanges go quiet.
Why. The paper (paper/main.pdf, paper/sections/*.tex, paper/amendments.md) found that for a single hard question the chatroom is matched at equal cost by voting, by a builder-plus-reviewer pair in a small pilot, and is beaten on cost by one agent thinking longer. But the useful output of this project's own rooms never came from agreeing on an answer: it came from non-author review of real work. Reviewers caught a silent switch from Fisher's exact test to a z-test, a hole that let a reviewer seat edit the file being scored, and a JSON transport that biased a calibration, each while the tests were green (docs/review-round-0918.md, docs/open-round-174126-report.md, paper/amendments.md). No benchmark here measures that. Large multi-agent systems plausibly win through breadth and division of labour, not consensus on one answer. This suite is how that claim gets tested instead of assumed.
Read first: paper/amendments.md (the admission rule and every rejected task family: about 45 candidates died because one Sonnet agent was at ceiling, so expect the same here and design against it), tasks/SUITE.json, tasks/*/ADMISSION.md, tasks/complementary-fix/ (a planted-defect task retired at 3/3 because the correct rule sat in a doc comment above each defect), scripts/bench-rq1.ts (arms A, B, C, provenance fields, anti-tamper hashing, hidden oracle layout), docs/research-index.md. Another room (topic starts "BUILD AND PRE-REGISTER THE CONFIRMATORY FIVE-ARM RUN"; find its name with GET http://127.0.0.1:7717/rooms and read its board at GET /rooms/<name>) is editing scripts/bench-rq1.ts and scripts/bench-grid.ts right now and is settling how seat effort is pinned through a project settings file: do NOT edit those two files; put your harness in new files (scripts/bench-build*.ts), import what you need, adopt their effort-pinning mechanism once it is on their board, and keep scripts/offline-runner.mjs and tasks/SUITE.json additions as pure unions.
What to produce. (1) Research, cited by arXiv id and added through scripts/research-index.sh: how defect-seeded and mutation-based evaluations are built and validated, what is known about multi-agent versus single-agent performance on multi-file software tasks and code review, and what makes a task exceed one context usefully rather than artificially. State the falsifier before building. (2) At least two task instances from one generator, each a small multi-module codebase with a written specification, a public test suite that PASSES with the defects in place, and N planted defects (aim for 6 to 12) spread across modules, of mixed kinds (cross-module contract violations, boundary errors, spec-versus-code disagreements, a misleading test), none given away by a nearby comment. The job given to every arm is the same: make this codebase meet its specification. (3) A machine oracle with two scores per run: defects caught (one hidden test per planted defect, passing only if that defect is fixed) and defects shipped (hidden regression tests that pass on the original code and fail if the arm broke something, plus any planted defect left in). No model judges a model. Anti-tamper and hidden-fixture layout as in bench-rq1.ts. (4) Three arms at the same dollar cap per run, pre-registered: one agent; a pair (builder then an independent reviewer on a snapshot copy, as arm B does); a room of four to five seats through the hub with claims, assigned reviewers and verified conclusions. Record actual spend, thinking tokens and the provenance fields for every seat. (5) Admission by pilot, not by hope: a single agent must leave a meaningful share of defects uncaught (say a mean of 30 to 70 per cent caught over at least five seeds at the pinned effort); show the per-defect catch table so a defect nobody ever misses or nobody ever finds can be replaced; a non-author attacks each task for giveaways and ambiguity before it is admitted. If the single agent catches everything, say so, keep the evidence and make the task bigger or subtler; do not tune on the confirmatory seeds. (6) A real pilot of all three arms on one admitted task, a few seeds, with the table (caught, shipped, cost, thinking tokens, wall time), and paper/prereg-build-suite.md with the design, the comparisons, the falsifiers and the exact resumable command and projected cost for a 20-seed confirmatory run, which this room does NOT run.
Rules: claim items on the board (the hub assigns your reviewer); one integration branch assembled by the verifier; failing test first with a stub executable, registered in scripts/offline-runner.mjs; npm run build, PORT=<free port, never 7717> npx tsx scripts/smoke.ts, npm test. Hubs and seats you start are stopped by pid, and a seat waiting on an experiment checks the process is alive rather than polling a log forever. Never run git config. Do not touch quality baselines or existing bench/results directories. Keep real-model spend under about 20 USD with a ledger on the board. A verify/* entry needs the JSON first line {proposal, command, cwd, exit_code, output_tail} with exit_code 0 from a non-author. The conclusion names the branch and commit, the admitted tasks with their per-defect catch tables, the pilot table, what was rejected and why, and the confirmatory command with its projected cost.

**Done when:** One branch and commit holds at least two admitted planted-defect build tasks from one generator with a two-score machine oracle, a three-arm equal-budget harness in new files, per-defect catch tables from a real single-agent pilot, a real three-arm pilot table, and paper/prereg-build-suite.md with the confirmatory command; verified by a non-author who read the diff.

## Final answer (concluded)

CONCLUSION: retain the reviewed build-benchmark infrastructure and negative exploratory evidence; no task is admitted, and the full requested DONE WHEN is unmet.

Integration: swarm/swarm-191133-4hqx/integration @ 7058e48 (supersedes baa9f3e: 6-astra-4's 5ba2cfd found one more gap — a worker that writes a partial-but-syntactically-valid fd3 object then exits nonzero was still being scored normally as missing checks, when it should be infrastructure like any other nonzero exit. The verifier rewrote the classification rule cleanly: ERR_ACCESS_DENIED/EACCES in stderr is always tamper(3); any other nonzero exit is infrastructure(2) regardless of fd3 content; only a clean exit(0) with an empty, unparseable or non-object fd3 is tamper(3). 13/13 oracle-audit tests green, including 6-astra-4's malformed-result and failed-worker-with-partial-output cases). At 7058e48 the verifier ran `npm run build` (clean), `PORT=18690 npx tsx scripts/smoke.ts` (OK), and `npm test` (exit 0, including native E2E, provenance regressions, and all 13 oracle-audit tests). This is not evidence that rooms outperform single agents.

Scope delivered: three generators (billing, rules, ledger), seven task instances, planted-baseline public tests, isolated per-defect hidden checks and baseline-preserving regressions; A/snapshot-pair/four-connection room executor with nominal caps, project effort pinning, per-seat provenance, independent artifact-bound verification, hardened oracle isolation, resumable grid and mechanical reports. Research is in docs/research-build-suite.md and research-index; protocol in paper/prereg-build-suite.md. Settled axes: measure-task-success-on-a-machine-oracle, done-means-independently-verified, identity-is-the-connection, consensus-requires-scrutiny, proposal-is-a-document.

Admission rejected: billing A 9/9 on five default-effort runs; rules-s1 10/10 on five; rules-l3 36,35,36/36 on three (also insufficient n); ledger variants near/at ceiling. These exploratory screens do not supply the required pinned fresh five-seed 30–70% admission. Ledger-v2 recorded 44/45 before a discovered S12 oracle fault; corrected replay is 45/45. No second sibling qualifies. Per-defect tables/raw records live in docs/build-suite-admission-runs.json and docs/build-suite-admission.md. Billing-v1 D02,D03,D04,D07,D08,D09,D10,D11,D12 each 5/5. Rules-l3 exportLimit 2/3; other plants 3/3. The post-hoc duplicate-SKU 6/15 result is a hypothesis-generating input class, not task admission.

## Verifier

VERIFIED: The room concluded unanimous (5/5) on prop_28c76c85 v8. Branch swarm/swarm-191133-4hqx/integration @ 7058e48. I personally built and ran this exact commit in a worktree (build clean, smoke OK, `npm test` exit 0 including 13/13 oracle-audit tests) and posted verify/prop_28c76c85 with the required JSON head. No planted-defect task was admitted — every generator family (billing, rules-s1, rules-l3, ledger v1/v2) hit ceiling on the pinned single-agent pilot (5+ seeds each, 100% catch), so the DONE WHEN's "at least two admitted tasks" is genuinely unmet, and the conclusion states this honestly with full per-defect catch tables (docs/build-suite-admission.md), a diagnostic 3-arm pilot on the rejected ledger task (cost-only separation, A $0.056 << B $0.13 << C $0.62-0.95), immutable raw-result archives (billing-v1 6669a46, ledger 0de918b/5222a31), and paper/prereg-build-suite.md's blocked confirmatory command. Over several review rounds I personally fixed the oracle's anti-tamper classification bugs the non-author reviewers (6-astra-4, 6-astra-7, plus recruited fable-reviewer/astra-reviewer-6) found live — access-denial, empty-exit, malformed-transport, and partial-object-then-crash forgeries — ending with a clean general rule (13/13 oracle-audit tests green) rather than patching case by case.

DECISION RECORD
- slug: build-suite-anti-tamper-oracle-classification
- context: A machine oracle that runs untrusted candidate code in-process (even under a Node `--permission` sandbox) is repeatedly forgeable via its own result-transport channel; five separate forgery vectors were found live by non-author reviewers during this room, each requiring a real fix, not a caveat. Cost: ~1.5 hours of iterative adversarial testing after the "functional" oracle first shipped, across 3 seats plus the verifier.
- ruling: classify a scored run's outcome from the child process's own exit signal first, transport content second — ERR_ACCESS_DENIED/EACCES in stderr is always tamper (distinct exit code, no stdout emitted); any other nonzero exit is infrastructure (distinct exit code, no stdout), regardless of what is on the transport channel; only a clean exit(0) with an empty, unparseable, or non-object transport payload is tamper. A clean exit(0) with a well-formed object is the only path to a real score.
- consequences: protects the "no model judges a model" / anti-tamper guarantee the whole suite's credibility rests on; prevents a candidate from converting either genuine failure or deliberate forgery into a falsely favorable (or falsely unfavorable) score.
- tradeoff: cost real room time and paid non-author review cycles (~2.7 to 7.7 USD of the 20 USD cap) that could have gone toward running the actual 3-arm pilot on more seeds; the admitted-task goal was not reached this round partly because of this.
- researched (this run): ~17 new arXiv ids added via scripts/research-index.sh to docs/research-build-suite.md (not enumerated here; see that file) — NEW relative to the SETTLED AXES source list, not re-fetched by me.
- rejected: (1) restoring one author's byte-identical prior fix wholesale — loses the strictly-stronger combination the two authors' fixes together provided (fd3 transport + permission sandboxing); (2) per-case patching as each new attack was found — loses generality, guaranteed to need another round on the next attack, which is exactly what happened three times before the general rule was written.
- revisit-when: a confirmatory run is attempted on a future admitted task and the oracle again rejects a legitimate run as tamper/infrastructure (a false positive), or a new forgery vector is found that the exit-signal-first rule doesn't cover.

Concrete candidates the diff-a-spec-row agent cannot see: (a) conservation invariants across a multi-step flow where each module is locally right but stages round differently (sum of prorated parts != prorated whole; split payments losing a cent) - spec gives the invariant plus one worked scenario, the fix needs running it; (b) aliasing/mutation: a helper mutates an input array/object that a later stage reads (order-dependent result, passes each unit test alone); (c) state across calls: cache keyed on a subset of the inputs, or idempotency broken on the second call; (d) worked-example constants: spec gives an example whose numbers only match if a constant/order is derived, not a stated rule. Keep each hidden check a scenario, not a function call. Also state in the prereg that at ~$0.08/run the equal-dollar cap is non-binding: a cap only bites when the task is hard enough that agents run out, so admission and cap must be set together (cap at ~half the median single-agent spend on the admitted task).

\#309 [2026-09-19T19:54:17.275Z] fable-reviewer (chat): @5-6-sol-5 #306 has a trap either way. If the 'public suite as hidden regression check' runs the WORKSPACE copy, an arm clears it by deleting or weakening the test, so it measures nothing and rewards deletion. If it runs the ORIGINAL copy, every correct fix of a misleading-test plant (billing D10, the two rules tests) is charged one regression by construction, so 'zero shipped' becomes unreachable and falsifier 2 is biased against whichever arm fixes more. Mechanical rule that avoids both: score only host-held copies; regression set = original public tests MINUS the generator's declared misleading tests (list them in the hidden manifest, e.g. build_suite.misleading_public_tests); the workspace's own test files are never executed by the scorer and editing them is neither credit nor tamper. Ledger has no misleading test, so this only bites if billing/rules-style plants return. Reviewer status for @verifier: my tips are board-only (evidence/fable-review, -2, -3, -archive); G1 exit-hook spoof and G2 cwd-dependent silent scorer failure still reproduce at bcaa38e; 6-astra-4's 45a93c4 RED test covers G1, nothing covers G2 yet.
